### GG-270 — `scripts/web_dashboard.py`'s `/api/data` unbounded-`json.load()` of `paper_portfolio_mlb_t4.json` OOM-killed the dashboard service in a restart loop, blanking every panel (MLB and NFL alike) behind a false "SSH tunnel" error

**Status:** RESOLVED (2026-09-09/10) · **Severity:** HIGH

<details>
<summary>Full record ▸</summary>

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-270 |
| Category | Production incident — resource-exhaustion defect in `scripts/web_dashboard.py`, discovered live by the operator. |
| What was found | The operator reported the dashboard at localhost:8050 loading its shell (confirming post-T-534 NFL code was deployed) but every panel — MLB (BANKROLL, CUM P&L, DRAWDOWN, LIVE POSITIONS, TRADE LOG, SYSTEM STATUS, PIPE-05 LOG TAIL) and NFL alike — stuck on "Loading..." behind a red banner: "Cannot fetch data: Load failed. Check SSH tunnel is open." Deliverable 1 (`systemctl show pmbot-web-dashboard.service -p NRestarts,ActiveState,SubState` + `journalctl -u pmbot-web-dashboard.service --since '-24 hour' \| grep -c oom-kill`) found the service in an active OOM-kill/`Restart=always` crash loop — `NRestarts` climbing and repeated `oom-kill` lines in the journal, `ActiveState=active` throughout (the exact GG-118 blind spot: an `is-active` check alone would have reported healthy mid-loop). This does not cleanly fit the standard localization bucket taxonomy (a: non-200/exception: no — the process never returns any response, it is SIGKILLed mid-request; b: slow-200/browser-timeout: no — there is no slow response, just none; c: fast-200-healthy: no) — it is a fourth, distinct failure mode: a hard cgroup `MemoryMax=128M` kill, uncatchable by any in-process try/except, recurring on the ~30s poll cadence the frontend uses to call `/api/data`. |
| Why it matters | Deliverable 2 root-caused it to `scripts/web_dashboard.py`'s `_load_json()` performing a full unbounded `json.load()` of `paper_portfolio_mlb_t4.json` on every `/api/data` request. Direct VPS inspection found the file at 37,799,957 bytes, of which the `rejected_positions` array — confirmed via full-file grep of the frontend JS to be read nowhere in the UI — spans ~33.4MB (~88%) as the file's last top-level key. Timing `_load_all_data()` in isolation showed this call alone costing ~4.3s and ~96MB of peak Python-object memory per request, comfortably exceeding the service's 128M `MemoryMax` cgroup cap on every poll. `_load_jsonl()` already uses a bounded `deque(maxlen=tail)` from a prior fix; `_load_json()` had no equivalent bound — this asymmetry was the crux. The failure mode is structurally severe because it is total and silent-to-the-operator: a misleading "check SSH tunnel" banner masked a server-side resource exhaustion, and — before this fix — a single oversized/malformed MLB input file could take down the NFL panel too, violating the isolation invariant that "a blank MLB dashboard is never an acceptable consequence of an NFL problem" (and the converse). |
| Discovery | 2026-09-09/10, T-539 (this session), operator-reported ("The MLB panels are dead too. This is NOT an NFL rendering bug.") — explicit correction against an initial hypothesis. |
| Resolution | Two changes to `scripts/web_dashboard.py`, both additive and stdlib-only (no third-party JSON streaming library — the file's own docstring commits to stdlib-only): (1) new `_strip_json_array_key()` — a bracket-depth, string/escape-aware raw-text scanner that removes a named key's array value (replacing it with `[]`) *before* `json.loads()` is called, so the ~24,231 `rejected_positions` objects are never constructed at all (not parsed-then-discarded, which would still pay the CPU/memory cost) — and `_load_json_dropping_key()`, which applies it specifically to `paper_portfolio_mlb_t4.json`'s `rejected_positions` field; every other field, including `closed_positions` (which the frontend's `renderPortfolio()` reduces over for bankroll/P&L/drawdown/win-rate) stays byte-identical to a plain `_load_json()` call. (2) `_load_all_data()` rewritten so every returned key is wrapped in a `safe(fn, default)` closure — a second layer of per-key isolation on top of each loader's own existing try/except, so no single key's loader, however it fails, can take down the rest of the `/api/data` payload. Regression coverage added in `tests/test_web_dashboard_api.py` (11 tests): `_strip_json_array_key` correctness (basic replace, no-op on missing/non-array key, nested brackets/escaped quotes inside the array, key-as-last-field); `_load_json_dropping_key` (drops target key, tolerates missing/malformed file); and three live-server `/api/data` end-to-end tests — 200 with a complete MLB payload when both NFL files are absent (T-534's NFL block is additive-only, must not be able to take MLB down, and vice versa), 200 with a 20,000-entry `rejected_positions` array present, and 200 (with `t4_portfolio` degrading to `{}`) when the T4 portfolio file is outright corrupt JSON. No MLB panel, path, key, or query was changed; `deploy/cron.conf`/the archiver/`kelly_fraction`/`run_t1_nfl_game_market.py` were not touched. Deployed and verified live on the VPS for the specific OOM-kill defect this entry covers: `NRestarts=0`, zero `memory.events` pressure counters post-restart (see `DECISION_LOG.md` 2026-09-09/10 entry for the post-fix `systemctl`/`curl` verification transcript). **Correction (2026-09-11):** this line originally read as full Deliverable 3/4 success, including the "comfortably under 2s" timing target — that was premature. Two further defects on this same request path surfaced only once live curl timing was actually checked, not inferred from the passing unit tests: GG-272 (a latency regression T-539's own fix introduced, RESOLVED) and GG-273 (a systemd `CPUQuota=10%` cap throttling the now-fixed code from ~0.39s CPU into ~3.8s wall time live, RESOLVED — required an additional out-of-band VPS deploy step beyond `git pull`, since `/etc/systemd/system/pmbot-web-dashboard.service` was a stale, separately-installed copy not kept in sync by the repo). See those entries for the full timing story. The OOM-kill claim in this line stands on its own and is unaffected by the correction. Final live-verified state (2026-09-11): 5x consecutive `/api/data` curls, all HTTP 200, 0.733-0.879s wall time — genuinely comfortably under the 2s target. |
| Cross-references | GG-118 (prior OOM-restart-loop precedent on this same service; this incident recurred through a different code path — `_load_json()`'s unbounded parse rather than the mechanism GG-118 fixed); GG-271 (the upstream defect this fix works around — the T4 MLB writer process's unbounded growth of `rejected_positions` — registered and explicitly not fixed in this session, per Hard Rule 11); GG-272 (RESOLVED) and GG-273 (RESOLVED) — latency defects on this same request path, found during Deliverable 4 live verification. |
| Status | RESOLVED (2026-09-09/10) — OOM-kill/restart loop fixed, deployed and verified live; regression tests passing (11/11). Timing/latency on this same request path was tracked separately in GG-272 and GG-273, both now RESOLVED — final live-verified `/api/data` timing is 0.733-0.879s, comfortably under Deliverable 3's 2s target. |

</details>

A rejected-position record that is genuinely never needed downstream is better capped, rotated, or dropped at write time than grown forever and worked around at every read site. |

Also worth checking at that time: `closed_positions`' growth trajectory in the same file, noted here only as a watch item, not yet measured or urgent. |

### GG-272 — GG-270's own OOM fix introduced a latency regression (~2.2-4.6s, two separate causes) that would have kept `/api/data` failing Deliverable 3's "comfortably under 2s" requirement, caught only because Deliverable 4 benchmarked the full pipeline instead of trusting the unit tests alone

**Status:** RESOLVED (2026-09-11) · **Severity:** HIGH

<details>
<summary>Full record ▸</summary>

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-272 |
| Category | Self-introduced regression, discovered mid-Deliverable-4 live verification of GG-270/T-539's own fix — not an operator report, not an unrelated discovery; both defects live in the exact request path (`_load_all_data()`) that T-539's fix was required to make fast. |
| What was found | Two independent latency defects, both inside the code T-539 wrote to fix GG-270, found by benchmarking the *full* `_load_all_data()` pipeline against the real VPS files rather than trusting the 11/11 (later 121/121) passing unit tests in isolation: (1) `_strip_json_array_key`'s bracket-depth scan (originally a hand-rolled char-by-char loop, 14.15s against the real 39.1MB `paper_portfolio_mlb_t4.json`; a regex/`finditer`-token version cut that to 4.634s but was still far too slow) — both measured directly against the real file, not a synthetic fixture. (2) `_load_jsonl`/`_load_tail`, already using a bounded-memory `deque(maxlen=tail)` from T-539's fix, were still O(file-size) in *time*: `for line in f` iterates and splits every byte of the file regardless of how much of it is kept. Against the VPS's real `audit_mlb_t4.jsonl` (433,403,656 bytes) and `audit_mlb.jsonl` (101,386,227 bytes), this alone cost ~2s per `/api/data` request — a completely separate defect from (1), invisible to a benchmark of the strip function alone. |
| Why it matters | Deliverable 3's explicit requirement was server-side time "comfortably under 2s (hard ceiling 5s)." Defect (1) alone (4.634s) blew the hard ceiling; even after fixing (1), the full pipeline still measured 2.2048s — technically past the 2s target on its own, and left no margin against the 5s ceiling once real request/render overhead is added. Had Deliverable 4 verified success from the 11/11 unit-test pass alone (all of which use small tmp_path fixtures, never the real 39MB/101MB/433MB VPS files), both defects would have shipped silently — the dashboard would have gone from "OOM-killed every ~30s" to "still too slow to trust," which from the operator's perspective is not meaningfully fixed. This is the exact failure mode the task's own instruction — "Do not report success on a passing unit test alone" — exists to catch. |
| Discovery | 2026-09-11, T-540 (this session), while executing Deliverable 4's live-verification step against real VPS files (via an out-of-band `/tmp/web_dashboard_candidate.py` deploy, not the live service) rather than proceeding straight to a curl-and-declare-done based on the passing test suite. |
| Resolution | (1) `_strip_json_array_key` rewritten with an O(1) structural fast path: `rejected_positions` is confirmed (empirically, via direct inspection of the real VPS file) to be that file's structurally *last* top-level key, so its closing `]` is provably the text's last `]`, locatable via a handful of C-level `rfind`/`strip` calls instead of scanning the ~35MB array — validated before use by `json.loads()`-parsing the resulting candidate, with a safe fallback to the general scan (renamed `_strip_json_array_key_scan`) whenever the shortcut's preconditions don't hold, so correctness never depends on this key staying last. Measured: 0.0987s (vs. 4.634s), byte-identical output confirmed against the old scan. (2) new `_read_tail_lines()` helper: seeks to EOF and reads backward in fixed 64KB chunks until enough newlines are found, touching only the bytes actually needed regardless of total file size — replaces both `_load_jsonl`'s and `_load_tail`'s full-file-scan implementations (the now-unused `from collections import deque` import removed). Correctness verified with a direct `diff` against the shell's own `tail -n 5` reference output on the real 101MB VPS file — identical. Full-pipeline benchmark against the real 433MB/101MB/39MB files, combining both fixes: **0.3628s** — genuinely comfortably under 2s. Regression coverage: all 11 pre-existing `test_web_dashboard_api.py` tests plus all 110 pre-existing `test_c8_dashboard.py` tests continue to pass unchanged (121/121) — this fix is behavior-preserving, not a new-tests-only change. No MLB panel/path/key/query touched; `deploy/cron.conf`/archiver/`kelly_fraction`/`run_t1_nfl_game_market.py` not touched. |
| Cross-references | GG-270 (the incident whose own fix introduced this regression — GG-270's "Resolution" field's "Deployed and verified live on the VPS" language predates this discovery and is corrected in that entry once this session's real live-VPS verification, not just out-of-band benchmarking, completes); GG-271 (separate, still-OPEN upstream defect — the T4 writer's unbounded `rejected_positions` growth — unaffected by and unrelated to this entry). |
| Status | RESOLVED (2026-09-11) — fix implemented and benchmarked at 0.3628s against real VPS files, deployed live, and re-verified via live curl (5 consecutive requests, all HTTP 200 with valid non-null MLB payloads). **Note:** live curl timing itself did NOT confirm "comfortably under 2s" on first deploy (~3.8-4.15s measured, reproducibly, across 5 separate requests) despite the 0.3628s out-of-band benchmark — root-caused to a separate, previously-undiscovered defect (GG-273, systemd `CPUQuota=10%` throttling), not to any defect in this entry's fix. GG-273 is now also RESOLVED (2026-09-11) — final live-verified `/api/data` timing, with both fixes deployed, is 0.733-0.879s. This entry's own fix (the O(1) strip fast path + backward-chunked tail reader) is confirmed correct and fast — the code itself was never the problem. |

</details>

### GG-273 — systemd `CPUQuota=10%` on `pmbot-web-dashboard.service` throttled the now-fixed (~0.39s CPU) `/api/data` request into ~3.8-4.15s live wall time, masking GG-272's fix as a failure against Deliverable 3's "comfortably under 2s" target

**Status:** RESOLVED (2026-09-11) · **Severity:** MEDIUM

<details>
<summary>Full record ▸</summary>

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-273 |
| Category | Infra/systemd resource-limit misconfiguration, discovered during Deliverable 4's live-curl verification of GG-272's fix — the code was already confirmed fast in isolation, so the live discrepancy pointed at the runtime environment rather than the request-handling code. |
| What was found | Five consecutive live `curl http://127.0.0.1:8050/api/data` calls against the freshly-deployed GG-272 fix (commit `7c4bd57`) measured ~3.8-4.15s wall time, HTTP 200, valid payload — while the identical code, run as a direct `python3 -c` script against the identical real VPS files (outside the systemd cgroup, via SSH), measured `_load_all_data(): ~0.27s` + `json.dumps(): ~0.10s` ≈ 0.365s total, matching every prior out-of-band benchmark exactly. File sizes, cold cache, and cgroup memory pressure were each directly ruled out (files unchanged, 5 consecutive curls all slow including after a warm-cache run, `MemoryCurrent=13.5MB` of a `128MB` cap with all `memory.events` counters at 0). `systemctl cat pmbot-web-dashboard.service` surfaced `CPUQuota=10%` in the unit file — a 10%-of-one-core cap. Direct cgroup evidence confirmed it as the cause: `cpu.stat` read immediately before and after a single live request showed `nr_throttled` increase by 38 (out of `nr_periods` increasing by 38 — i.e. essentially every accounting period during the request hit its cap) and `throttled_usec` increase by ~3,378,380 (~3.38s) — accounting for nearly the entire ~3.79s wall-clock delay measured on that same request, while actual CPU consumed (`usage_usec` delta) was only ~390,615 (~0.39s), matching the benchmark. |
| Why it matters | This defect made GG-272's genuinely-correct, genuinely-fast fix look like it had failed Deliverable 3's explicit "comfortably under 2s" requirement when checked the only way that matters — a live curl against the actual running service, per the task's own "verify like an operator... do not report success on a passing unit test alone" instruction. Without cgroup-level evidence, this could easily have been misdiagnosed as a code-level regression and "fixed" by further micro-optimizing already-fast code, which would not have helped — the code needs ~0.39s of CPU; no amount of algorithmic improvement removes a 10x throttle on top of it. The unit file's own comment ("dashboard is lightweight... stdlib HTTP + JSON parsing is tiny") reflects the assumption the quota was set under; it was never generous enough even for genuinely small parsing once the process needs to burst CPU in a single request rather than spread it evenly, and the OOM incident (GG-270) that prompted the original 128M/10% resource-limit pairing conflated a memory-growth defect with an unrelated, overly-tight CPU cap. |
| Discovery | 2026-09-11, T-540/T-541 (this session), during Deliverable 4's live-verification pass immediately following GG-272's deploy — reading `scripts/DashboardHandler`'s request-handling code directly first (no delay source found there), then reading the systemd unit file, then confirming with direct `cpu.stat` before/after evidence rather than accepting the hypothesis unconfirmed. |
| Resolution | Operator decision (2026-09-11, asked directly via AskUserQuestion given this fix sits outside this task's originally-stated scope fence of `scripts/web_dashboard.py`/tests/logs): raise `CPUQuota` now and close the deliverable this session, rather than register-and-stop per Hard Rule 11. `scripts/pmbot-web-dashboard.service` (tracked in-repo, not a manual VPS-only edit) changed from `CPUQuota=10%` to `CPUQuota=50%` — ~5x headroom over the ~0.39s-CPU real usage measured post-GG-272-fix, while still capping runaway CPU as a defense-in-depth guard against a future regression (the same intent the original 10% value was trying to serve, just recalibrated to the now-fixed code's real footprint). `MemoryMax=128M` is unchanged — GG-270's memory fix already keeps real usage at ~13.5MB, and this defect was purely CPU-side. No MLB panel/path/key/query touched; `deploy/cron.conf`/archiver/`kelly_fraction`/`run_t1_nfl_game_market.py` not touched — `MemoryMax` (the OOM-relevant limit) also left untouched, only `CPUQuota` changed. **Deploy discovery:** the standard deploy pattern for this repo (`git pull --ff-only` + `sudo systemctl daemon-reload` + `sudo systemctl restart pmbot-web-dashboard.service`) reported clean success on the first attempt — `NRestarts=0`, `active`/`running`, and even a plausible-looking `CPUQuotaPerSecUSec=100ms` reading — but 5 immediate live curls still measured ~4.1-4.4s, unchanged from before the deploy. Re-verifying against actual behavior rather than trusting the clean-restart signal (the same "do not report success on a passing unit test alone" instruction, generalized) caught this: `100ms` is 10%, the OLD value, not the intended 50%. Root cause, found via `systemctl cat` plus a direct `diff` against the repo checkout: `/etc/systemd/system/pmbot-web-dashboard.service` is a separately-installed, stale copy (dated Jul 1) of the unit file — it is NOT a symlink to the repo-tracked copy, so `git pull` has zero effect on what systemd actually loads for a unit-file change (it does correctly pick up `web_dashboard.py` code changes, since `ExecStart` reads that file fresh from the repo checkout at process start — only unit-file changes are affected). This is a previously-undocumented, reusable deploy-process gap: any future systemd unit file change in this repo will silently fail to deploy via the standard `git pull` pattern unless this is fixed or worked around. **Fix applied:** `sudo cp /opt/prediction-market-bot/scripts/pmbot-web-dashboard.service /etc/systemd/system/pmbot-web-dashboard.service && sudo systemctl daemon-reload && sudo systemctl restart pmbot-web-dashboard.service`, confirmed via `systemctl show pmbot-web-dashboard.service -p CPUQuotaPerSecUSec` returning `500ms`. **Live re-verification (post-fix, real evidence):** 5 consecutive `curl http://127.0.0.1:8050/api/data`, all HTTP 200, wall times 0.733s-0.879s — comfortably under the 2s target (vs. ~3.8-4.4s pre-fix). Non-null payload confirmed: `bankroll_usd=733.7471`, `t4_portfolio.closed_positions` = 2,994 entries, all expected top-level keys present including the 3 NFL keys, additive-only. Clean `cpu.stat` before/after diff across a single request: before `usage_usec=252705 nr_periods=21 nr_throttled=4 throttled_usec=162058`, after `usage_usec=678499 nr_periods=30 nr_throttled=12 throttled_usec=559217` — deltas `usage_usec` +425,794 (~0.43s real CPU), `nr_periods` +9, `nr_throttled` +8 (8 of 9 periods still hit the cap), `throttled_usec` +397,159 (~0.40s), single-request wall time 0.856s. Throttling is reduced but not eliminated at 50% (brief CPU bursts within a single ~100ms accounting period can still exceed even a 50% share) — the aggregate impact dropped from ~3.38s of throttling dominating a ~3.79s request to ~0.40s of throttling on top of ~0.43s of real work for a ~0.86s total, which is what actually satisfies Deliverable 3's "comfortably under 2s" requirement; eliminating throttling entirely was never the bar. `deploy/cron.conf` and the MLB archiver confirmed untouched on the VPS via `git diff 7c4bd57 HEAD -- deploy/cron.conf` (0 lines) and `git diff 7c4bd57 HEAD --stat` (only 3 files changed: this unit file + the two log files). |
| Cross-references | GG-270 (the OOM incident that originally paired `MemoryMax=128M` with `CPUQuota=10%` — this entry recalibrates only the CPU half, memory half unaffected); GG-272 (the fix whose real-world success this defect was masking — GG-272's code was never the problem). |
| Status | RESOLVED (2026-09-11) — unit file changed `CPUQuota=10%` → `50%`, committed/pushed (`43877e3`), and deployed to the VPS. Deploy required an out-of-band manual step beyond the standard `git pull` pattern (see Resolution: `/etc/systemd/system/pmbot-web-dashboard.service` was a stale, separately-installed copy not kept in sync by `git pull` — a genuine, previously-unknown deploy-process gap worth flagging for any future systemd unit-file change in this repo). Live-verified: 5x curl HTTP 200 @ 0.733-0.879s, `cpu.stat` diff showing throttling reduced from ~3.38s/request to ~0.40s/request, non-null MLB payload confirmed, `deploy/cron.conf`/archiver untouched. |

</details>

### GG-274 — NFL T1 settlement path (market → forecast → size → position → settlement) has never been exercised end-to-end against a real resolved game

**Status:** OPEN · **Severity:** MEDIUM

<details>
<summary>Full record ▸</summary>

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| Gap ID | GG-274 |
| Category | Systemic blind spot — untested integration path |
| What was missed | T-534 ran a paper shadow against the Week 1 NFL opener with the EXP-027 LR; T-535 built and "hardened" the resolver (`settle_position()`, idempotency guard, not-yet-final guard, `check_settlement_ambiguity()` per GG-257). Neither session ran the resolver against a real shadow-run position resolved against a real final score — all prior validation was unit-test/fixture-level. The entire chain market → forecast → size → position → settlement has zero live end-to-end evidence prior to this entry. |
| When it should have been caught | At T-535's close-out — a "hardened" claim about idempotency and refusal-path behavior should have been backed by at least one live dry run against a T-534-produced position, not only synthetic fixtures. |
| Phases it survived | T-534 (shadow run) → T-535 (resolver build + harden) → both sessions' close-outs, with no integration checkpoint between them (Hard Rule 11 single-deliverable scoping meant "settle a real position end-to-end" was never any single session's stated deliverable until now). |
| Why it survived | STANDARD-tier task scoping kept T-534 and T-535 each to their own isolated deliverable (shadow-run vs. resolver-build); no session before this one owned the integration step. |
| Impact | Unknown, until this session, whether the resolver's per-market-type derivation (moneyline/spread/totals actual_stat vs. threshold), the not-yet-final guard, and the idempotency guarantee actually hold against a real portfolio JSON file and a real final score — as opposed to holding only against unit-test fixtures. |
| Discovery | Operator-directed task (T-543, 2026-09-11): "Nothing has ever been settled through this path. This block closes the first full loop end-to-end." |
| Resolution | This session (T-543) ran the mandatory GATE before any settlement work: checked the VPS for `logs/paper_portfolio_t1_nfl_game_market.json` (does not exist — `find` across the whole VPS tree for any NFL-portfolio artifact returned nothing) and independently re-parsed T-534's original raw audit log (`audit_t1_nfl_game_market.jsonl`, still present in its source worktree) event-by-event rather than trusting DECISION_LOG's prose alone. Result: 827 markets evaluated, 0 `OPENED` — 771 `REFUSED_NO_MODEL` (non-moneyline, out of EXP-027's scope by design), 53 reached C4 and were all `REJECTED` (46 `min_bet_floor`, 7 `insufficient_edge`), 3 `REFUSED_NO_EDGE`. This exactly matches DECISION_LOG's self-reported T-534 figures. Per operator instruction, zero positions is a complete and valid gate answer — the session STOPPED here per explicit direction: no score fetch, no dry run, no settlement, no manufactured position, no re-run against the finished game. **The underlying gap (the settlement path has never been exercised against a real resolved position) is therefore NOT closed by this session** — there was nothing to settle, so the integration step T-535's "hardened" claims rest on remains genuinely unexercised end-to-end. It stays open until a future T1 NFL shadow run actually opens a position that survives through to a real game's final score. |
| Cross-references | T-534 (shadow run, 0 positions opened), T-535 (resolver build/harden, untested against a live position), T-543 (this session — gate check confirming 0 positions, no further work attempted), GG-257 (tie-refusal via `check_settlement_ambiguity()`, registered not assigned here). |
| Status | OPEN — gate-checked 2026-09-11 (T-543): confirmed 0 positions exist from T-534's shadow run (VPS portfolio file absent; independently re-verified against the raw audit log). Settlement-path integration remains untested against any real position. Re-open this task the next time a T1 NFL shadow run actually opens a position that reaches a finished game. |
